title: 'Claim. Spell. Delegate.'

Last December, in a [rambling ode to Claude Opus 4.5](/2025/12/20/land-of-opus-and-glory), I joked that I’d keep using Claude Code to build stuff “until of course I forget what code looks like completely and have an identity crisis”.

Reader, I have forgotten what code looks like.

Between February and October this year, I typed **115 lines of code** for the word game I’ve been working on. Not 115 a day, or 115 a week. 115, total, and most of those were me nudging a margin by a few pixels or fixing a typo in the update log. Over the same period the codebase grew to around **334,000 lines**.

The game is called **[Gramlet](https://gramlet.co.uk)**, and if you’ve been reading this blog for a while you might remember its ancestor: [Anagramageddon](/2022/12/08/anagramageddon-fight-for-territory-and-find-anagrams-as-you-battle-against-the-clock), a scrappy little two-player game I built by hand back in 2022, with a 200-line JavaScript file I described at the time as “something of an abomination”. It sat more or less untouched for nearly three years. Then Claude Code on the web arrived, and last December I picked it back up, and things got slightly out of hand.

This article is my attempt to work out what, if anything, I can still take credit for.

### What even is Gramlet?

Gramlet is a daily word game. You claim letters on a grid, one at a time, always next to one you already own, and after each claim you spell a word from your growing bank of letters. That’s the core of it, and it’s the bit I came up with in 2022. Everything else has grown around it since, mostly by asking “what if?”. What if the clock was ticking? What if you couldn’t see the whole board? What if some letters were worth more than others? What if your opponent was a bot? Most of those questions turned into a game mode of their own. The one to start with is Volt, where your score is multiplied by a bar that drains while you think.

![Three phone screenshots of Gramlet. The first shows the Timeless grid, with a cluster of claimed letters in the top-left corner and the rest hidden under padlocks. The second shows the letter bank, where you spell a word from your claimed letters. The third shows Volt, with the surge bar running down the left-hand side of the grid.](./gramlet-screens.png)
_Timeless mid-game, spelling a word from the letter bank, and Volt with its surge bar_

Give it a go at [gramlet.co.uk](https://gramlet.co.uk) – no account needed. My wife has been its chief tester since January. She finds a lot of the bugs that only show up on an iPhone.

### Asking the robot about the robot

Here’s how I went about this. I asked Claude to analyse every single Claude Code session I’ve had on Gramlet, the whole git history and the GitHub pull requests, and to tell me honestly what I’d contributed. I am aware that AI has a taste for sycophancy, so I asked it to back every claim with evidence from the data.

Getting hold of the data took a bit of fiddling. My sessions live on Anthropic’s servers, and the only way Claude could read them from inside its sandbox was a hundred events at a time, through a tool only the model could call. Every page meant another trip through the model. It estimated a full extraction would cost around a billion tokens, and kept gently suggesting it might eat my entire weekly usage allowance. By the next morning I’d had enough:

> definitely not, please stop threatening to use all of my usage, that is never going to be an option

In the end I sat down at my laptop, opened Claude Desktop, and it wrote a script that ran in my Chrome tab and pulled down the lot: **1,619 sessions, 6.4 GB of transcripts**, and the download itself used no tokens at all.

### The numbers

Here’s the headline stuff, from December 2025 (when Claude got involved) to 12 October 2026:

-   **9,918 commits** to master, about 2,230 pull requests and about 950 GitHub issues
-   **1,619 Claude Code sessions**, on 271 of 314 days
-   **9,767 messages** typed by me, adding up to about **441,000 words**, roughly five novels’ worth
-   **About 99%** of the code added since December was written by an AI
-   **42%** of the codebase is now tests. In 2022 it was zero

So the AI wrote the code. The more interesting question is what I was doing with all those words.

### Five versions of me

Reading back through ten months of my own messages was a strange experience, a bit like finding an old diary, if your diary was mostly you swearing at a robot about a word game. But a pretty clear story emerged, in five chapters.

**Pair programmer (December to January).** I was still a developer, just with a very fast colleague. I ran the app on my Windows laptop, pasted stack traces back into the chat, tweaked CSS by hand and wrote some of the end-to-end tests myself. My prompts were proper specifications, a median of 33 words a message, and the AI was mostly doing what it was told. Not that I was always patient while it did:

> Literally just write the f\*cking code already there is no way to exit plan mode, so can you just get on with it

**Product manager (February to April).** I stopped committing code around the middle of February and didn’t really notice. I was too busy arguing. This was the era of me checking every claim it made, and of a fairly relaxed attitude to risk:

> I can’t be arsed with a follow up pr / migration to remove the allowlist, it isn’t that big a deal if sh\*t f\*cks up as we’ve only got about five users

It was also when I started asking it to review its own work. On the last day of January I told it: “as of this moment, right now, you are no longer the code author, you are the code interrogator”. By May that prompt had become a permanent “engineering lead” skill, and by August a hook that physically blocked Claude from finishing a pull request until it had interrogated itself. A little bit of me lives on in that hook.

**Reviewer on a phone (May to July).** Claude Code works on mobile, and I discovered that I could ship features from bed. In March, 13% of my sessions were started from my phone. By July it was 84%. My messages shrank accordingly, to a median of about 15 words, and on 6 June I first typed the phrase that would come to define our relationship: **“merge if you’re happy”**. I have now typed that, or “merge when you’re happy”, 462 times. My prompts also got stranger. A real message, from 16 June:

> be agentic, octopus intelligence, set up agents, subagents, agents within agents, mastermind this sh\*t - I don’t need to tell you what to do, you are a genius

![A line chart of the running total of times I typed “merge if you’re happy” or “merge when you’re happy”, from the first on 6 June to 462 by October. It climbs steeply through June, July and August, then flattens in September.](./merge-if-happy.svg)
_“Merge if you’re happy”, a running total_

**Factory owner (August).** August was absurd: 2,818 commits in a single month. I let sessions fix and merge small problems on their own, and my prompts became correspondingly high-level. Here is one from 18 August, in its entirety:

> fix broken sh\*t

At one point I had twelve sessions running at once on a Saturday afternoon. I also tried OpenAI’s Codex for three days, having twice threatened Claude with it in the spring, once as a rival that would check its work and once as its replacement.

**Editor (September to now).** Fewer sessions, much bigger ones. My median message is now 11 words. The record for a whole session was set back in August. Here is the entirety of my side of it, in which Claude fixed the backend tests and merged the fix:

> fix backend tests
>
> merge

Four words, 21 minutes apart.

### Whose ideas were they, anyway?

This was the bit I actually cared about. Code authorship is a pretty rubbish measure of credit for a game. Nobody thinks Dumbledore built Hogwarts. He just runs it, in the sense that children keep nearly dying there and Ofsted somehow never visits. Still his school. The question is who came up with the stuff that makes Gramlet _Gramlet_.

#### The name

The most obvious place to start is the name on the box.

Over two days in a Claude chat, Claude offered around 500 names; I sent 37 messages, had 14 checked for clashes, and settled it with mock logos in Gemini.

#### Every change, traced

The name came out of one chat over two days. The rest of the game arrived in smaller pieces over ten months, which takes a different approach.

Gramlet’s update log lists every change players have been told about: 220 of them, from January to October. Asking who had each idea first turned out to be too blunt, because most ideas went back and forth between us before they shipped. So for the 113 bigger changes, I traced the whole conversation behind each one: every message that shaped what shipped, 822 of them. Two separate Claude instances, not told who had said what, then rated how much each message mattered, from a detail to the move that defined the change. A change’s credit is split by those ratings. The 107 minor changes go whole to whoever raised them, and a friend, a player or my wife counts as someone else. A few ideas predate the log altogether: claiming a letter and then spelling a word was already the turn in my 2022 code, the letter bank came out of a Claude chat on 23 December, and Gemini drew the logo to my brief.

A smaller 6x6 grid with every letter showing, no clock and no pressure, no accounts, and rude words are never accepted (the daily grid even steers clear of letters that could spell them). Teachers can run it with a whole class: the class board keeps everyone's best words hidden while the timer runs and reveals them when time is up, with a Reset board button for a fresh go. Turn a tablet on its side and it lays out side by side, with the active player's panel lit up. The Volt hub's scorecard tab has been redesigned to show these at a glance, and Share now creates a matching Volt image.", "o": "will", "a": "play", "w": 2, "b": "I asked for Volt scorecard slides in May and restarted the design in June.", "sh": {"will": 0.611, "claude": 0.389}, "mv": [{"k": "will", "via": "", "act": "Proposes", "sum": "Asks to turn on scorecard slides for Voltage.", "q": "I want to turn on scorecard / slides for the game mode Voltage soon", "qs": "", "d": "3 May", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 2.0}, {"k": "will", "via": "", "act": "Changes it", "sum": "Restarts the plan and asks to craft each slide rather than port Timeless's.", "q": "I don't just want to go ahead and build everything at once - I want to really craft this slideshow", "qs": "", "d": "18 Jun", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 2.0}, {"k": "claude", "via": "", "act": "Proposes", "sum": "Proposes The Cut: five rounds shown, two weakest fall away, three sum to the score.", "q": "The Cut – the signature. Your five rounds on screen, then the two weakest visibly *fall away*", "qs": "", "d": "18 Jun", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 3.0}, {"k": "claude", "via": "", "act": "Proposes", "sum": "Proposes a Surge slide built on average multiplier and surge bonus.", "q": "As it fills, the hero multiplier ticks up to your average (2.13× here)", "qs": "", "d": "18 Jun", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 1.0}, {"k": "will", "via": "", "act": "Turns down", "sum": "Rejects bonus framing: the multiplier only falls, so it is points not lost.", "q": "given the multiplier only goes down, it's more like how many points can you avoid losing", "qs": "", "d": "18 Jun", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 1.0}, {"k": "will", "via": "", "act": "Changes it", "sum": "Asks for a dynamic, punning caption for each score band.", "q": "I want 10 alternative pieces of content, one for each of the deciles", "qs": "", "d": "18 Jun", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 1.0}, {"k": "claude", "via": "", "act": "Proposes", "sum": "Recommends Trend as the third slide: result, skill, trajectory.", "q": "Recommendation: Trend (your score over the last ~180 days).", "qs": "", "d": "19 Jun", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 2.0}, {"k": "will", "via": "", "act": "Changes it", "sum": "Adds a word-length slide; defines surge and length as percentages of an ideal.", "q": "for the words one, it should be the actual points the user scored, divided by the points the user would have scored if they'd played the longest word possible", "qs": "", "d": "19 Jun", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 2.0}, {"k": "claude", "via": "", "act": "Offers options", "sum": "Offers five hub scorecard designs: receipt, bento, round rail, trading card, editorial.", "q": "Bento dashboard – a modern tile grid: a big score+rank hero tile, two mini-meters", "qs": "", "d": "19 Jun", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 1.0}, {"k": "will", "via": "", "act": "Chooses", "sum": "Picks the bento dashboard for the hub scorecard.", "q": "Bento dashboard please", "qs": "", "d": "19 Jun", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 2.0}, {"k": "will", "via": "", "act": "Chooses", "sum": "Limits the bento scorecard to Volt for now.", "q": "Volt-only it is.", "qs": "Claude, reporting my choice", "d": "19 Jun", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 1.0}]}, {"d": "20 June 2026", "t": "You can now choose which scorecard slides appear automatically after a game, set separately for Timeless and Volt, under Manage in your profile.", "o": "will", "a": "other", "w": 1, "b": "I spotted the slide settings needed updating and asked to split them by mode.", "sh": {"will": 1.0}, "e": [{"r": "First raised", "who": "Me", "d": "20 June 2026", "s": "Claude Code", "q": "only other thing I've just thought of is the Manage page where we allow users to specify which scorecard slides they see"}, {"r": "Decided", "who": "Me", "d": "20 June 2026", "s": "Claude Code", "q": "I think we need two separate settings for clarity - Timeless scorecard slides and Volt scorecard slides"}]}, {"d": "20 June 2026", "t": "Triplet (in Beta) has been rebuilt. Each day still sets you 3 challenges - 1 path, 1 pick, and 1 word - but the game now enforces them as you play instead of checking at the end: any letter or word that would break a rule is greyed out or rejected. There's no way to fail, so the game becomes squeezing out the highest score while the 3 rules box you in. Tap a greyed-out letter to see which rule is blocking it, and undo or restart any time.", "o": "will", "a": "rules", "w": 2, "b": "I asked for Triplet to enforce its challenges live, and chose how disabled letters show.", "sh": {"will": 0.467, "claude": 0.533}, "mv": [{"k": "will", "via": "", "act": "Proposes", "sum": "Proposes the game enforce challenges live, disabling violating moves; curate a smaller set.", "q": "what I want instead: the game reacts live, physically preventing you … violating a challenge.", "qs": "", "d": "17 Jun", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 3.0}, {"k": "claude", "via": "", "act": "Proposes", "sum": "Draws the line: keep locally enforceable constraints, drop achievement-style goals.", "q": "That single rule (\"keep locally-enforceable constraints, drop achievements\") is what shrinks 90 → a tight, reactive-native set", "qs": "", "d": "17 Jun", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 2.0}, {"k": "claude", "via": "", "act": "Offers options", "sum": "Puts three design calls that change the build before planning it.", "q": "Before I draft the curated list + implementation plan, three calls are genuinely yours to make because they change the build", "qs": "", "d": "17 Jun", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 1.0}, {"k": "will", "via": "", "act": "Chooses", "sum": "Settles completion as success, undo for dead ends, and keeping path/pick/word slots.", "q": "Locked in: completion = success (no fail state – a constrained scoring puzzle), lean on undo for dead-ends, and keep the path/pick/word triptych.", "qs": "Claude, reporting my choice", "d": "17 Jun", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 2.0}, {"k": "claude", "via": "", "act": "Decides while building", "sum": "Curates 13 path, pick and word rules each, with a reason shown for greyed cells.", "q": "Each carries a `lockHint` for the \"why is this greyed out\" message.", "qs": "", "d": "17 Jun", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 2.0}, {"k": "will", "via": "", "act": "Proposes", "sum": "Proposes colour-linking each disabled cell to the challenge type that disables it.", "q": "it would be nice to somehow visually link disabled letters / cells to the challenge that disables them", "qs": "", "d": "18 Jun", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 1.0}, {"k": "claude", "via": "", "act": "Offers options", "sum": "Offers corner pips, glow rings or split fills, with one colour per challenge type.", "q": "A – Corner pips & tint. Each disabled cell wears a tiny pip in the rule's colour", "qs": "", "d": "18 Jun", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 1.0}, {"k": "will", "via": "", "act": "Chooses", "sum": "Chooses corner pips and tint.", "q": "corner pips and tint please. designs look great", "qs": "", "d": "19 Jun", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 1.0}, {"k": "claude", "via": "", "act": "Decides while building", "sum": "Keeps rule-blocked cells tappable to explain themselves; word rules shown in a tab above entry.", "q": "Challenge-blocked cells need to stay tappable (they explain themselves) while out-of-reach cells stay inert.", "qs": "", "d": "19 Jun", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 2.0}]}, {"d": "20 June 2026", "t": "Triplet is starting fresh with the rebuild, so your games from the old version now live under a separate mode called Triplet Legacy. You can still find and replay them from your play history and the See all games page - they show the challenges you actually played that day, with a tick or cross for each.", "o": "will", "a": "other", "w": 1, "b": "I raised how old Triplet games would display and proposed the Legacy rename.", "sh": {"will": 1.0}, "e": [{"r": "First raised", "who": "Me", "d": "20 June 2026", "s": "Claude Code", "q": "I've just had a thought - old Triplet games are gonna need to be handled differently"}, {"r": "Decided", "who": "Me", "d": "20 June 2026", "s": "Claude Code", "q": "in the same way did a Volt Legacy rename, I wonder if we rename past Triplet games to Triplet Legacy"}]}, {"d": "20 June 2026", "t": "Backing music now plays in every mode. The Beta modes - Free Play, Triplet, Abyss, Parity, Roots, and Snake - used to be silent; they now play the same relaxed track the main modes do, from the moment the grid appears.", "o": "will", "a": "play", "w": 1, "b": "I noticed Triplet was silent and asked for music in every mode.", "sh": {"will": 1.0}, "e": [{"r": "First raised", "who": "Me", "d": "20 June 2026", "s": "Claude Code", "q": "oh also I have no music on Triplet, why? music should be on all game modes"}, {"r": "Context", "who": "Claude", "d": "20 June 2026", "s": "Claude Code", "q": "a deliberate \"beta modes are silent\" decision that your directive now overrides"}]}, {"d": "20 June 2026", "t": "VoltArena has a new beta twist called Boosters, which puts 3 special letters - gold (4x), silver (3x), and bronze (2x) - in the middle of the grid, showing through the fog from the first turn. Claim a word on one and that word scores its booster multiplier on top of your surge. Switch Boosters on when you challenge a friend - they see it's a Boosters match before they accept - or start a whole Grambots career on Boosters, where every fixture plays with them.", "o": "will", "a": "rules", "w": 2, "b": "I specified the bronze, silver and gold letters and picked the name Boosters from Claude's list.", "sh": {"will": 0.688, "claude": 0.312}, "mv": [{"k": "will", "via": "", "act": "Proposes", "sum": "Specifies VoltArena with bronze 2×, silver 3×, gold 4× letters, central and non-adjacent.", "q": "one letter somewhere on the grid is bronze (2x word score on claim turn), one is silver (3x) and one is gold (4x)", "qs": "", "d": "18 Jun", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 3.0}, {"k": "claude", "via": "", "act": "Offers options", "sum": "Asks whether multipliers stack with surge and whether the cue leaks through fog.", "q": "does the tile multiplier stack with the existing surge multiplier (gold 4× × surge 3× = 12×) or replace it?", "qs": "", "d": "18 Jun", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 1.0}, {"k": "will", "via": "", "act": "Chooses", "sum": "Has surge stack with the bonus; the cue shows through fog but not the letter.", "q": "surge does stack with bonus yes. and yes the cue shows better letter unfogged, but not the letter itself", "qs": "", "d": "18 Jun", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 2.0}, {"k": "claude", "via": "", "act": "Offers options", "sum": "Offers three cue styles, recommending a glow with a ×N chip.", "q": "C (glow + ×N chip) is the strongest default", "qs": "", "d": "19 Jun", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 1.0}, {"k": "will", "via": "", "act": "Names the problem", "sum": "Asks how a bonus letter looks after it is claimed and in the post-game view.", "q": "because if the bonus only applies on the turn you claim it, it shouldn't look like a bonus cell once claimed, right", "qs": "", "d": "19 Jun", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 1.0}, {"k": "claude", "via": "", "act": "Proposes", "sum": "Separates an 'available' chip with glow from a 'captured' medal once claimed.", "q": "Available = bright square ×N chip + glow (bottom-right), neutral tile.", "qs": "", "d": "19 Jun", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 1.0}, {"k": "will", "via": "", "act": "Changes it", "sum": "Builds it into VoltArena challenges and makes a whole Grambots career bonus or not.", "q": "on Grambots, an entire career / save would either be no bonus tiles, or bonus tiles", "qs": "", "d": "19 Jun", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 2.0}, {"k": "will", "via": "", "act": "Changes it", "sum": "Requires bonus letters to show during first-letter selection.", "q": "letter bonuses should be visible during selection phase", "qs": "", "d": "19 Jun", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 1.0}, {"k": "claude", "via": "", "act": "Offers options", "sum": "Offers placements for the toggle, recommending a challenge composer and career-type cards.", "q": "My pairing: friends → B (composer), career → A (type cards)", "qs": "", "d": "19 Jun", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 1.0}, {"k": "will", "via": "", "act": "Changes it", "sum": "Keeps the ×N mark always; border and glow only while the bonus is unspent.", "q": "the \"x2\" / \"x3\" / \"x4\" text in the bottom right corner should ALWAYS be there", "qs": "", "d": "20 Jun", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 1.0}, {"k": "claude", "via": "", "act": "Offers options", "sum": "Offers eight names, favouring 'Charged', with 'Boosters' among them.", "q": "Boosters – clearest \"this multiplies your score\" read; gamey.", "qs": "", "d": "20 Jun", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 1.0}, {"k": "will", "via": "", "act": "Chooses", "sum": "Names the feature Boosters.", "q": "boosters please", "qs": "", "d": "20 Jun", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 1.0}]}, {"d": "20 June 2026", "t": "You can now give a Grambots career its own avatar - pick an emoji when you start a new career, or tap your avatar on the My career page to change it any time.", "o": "will", "a": "other", "w": 1, "b": "I suggested a chosen Grambots avatar; Claude filed it and picked the symbol set.", "sh": {"will": 1.0}, "e": [{"r": "First raised", "who": "Me", "d": "16 June 2026", "s": "Claude Code", "q": "I wonder if we can choose our own avatar at the beginning of a Grambots save - give our profile a bit more identity"}, {"r": "Decided", "who": "Me", "d": "16 June 2026", "s": "Claude Code", "q": "key thing would be to ensure that that emoji is then not used by other bots"}, {"r": "Context", "who": "Claude", "d": "16 June 2026", "s": "Claude Code", "q": "the 12 options are all non-animal symbols, so none collide with the 63 bot faces."}]}, {"d": "19 June 2026", "t": "Your Volt profile stats now include Average top round score - the average of your best single round across your games, alongside the existing Top round score.", "o": "will", "a": "other", "w": 1, "b": "I asked for an average best-round stat on the Volt profile.", "sh": {"will": 1.0}, "e": [{"r": "First raised", "who": "Me", "d": "19 June 2026", "s": "Claude Code", "q": "add \"average best round score\" to Volt stats on user profile"}, {"r": "Decided", "who": "Me", "d": "19 June 2026", "s": "Claude Code", "q": "we need to choose between \"top\" and \"best\" for consistency."}]}, {"d": "19 June 2026", "t": "The backing music now starts the moment the grid appears and plays right through your game, across all the main modes - including Timeless, which used to be silent. Bots are real personas: avatars, skill levels, playstyles, the lot.", "qs": "Claude, reporting my choice", "d": "13 Jun", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 1.0}, {"k": "will", "via": "", "act": "Chooses", "sum": "Chooses a living ladder: bots promote, relegate and keep career records.", "q": "Locked: living ladder. Bots have full careers – they promote, relegate, hold profiles", "qs": "Claude, reporting my choice", "d": "13 Jun", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 2.0}, {"k": "will", "via": "", "act": "Chooses", "sum": "Makes the whole ladder visible, not just the player's division.", "q": "Whole ladder it is – depth is the headline, so let people *see* the whole mountain.", "qs": "Claude, reporting my choice", "d": "13 Jun", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 1.5}, {"k": "will", "via": "", "act": "Chooses", "sum": "No session gate: a campaign waits indefinitely, with only a gentle nudge.", "q": "Settled – no gate, waits forever, toothless nudge only.", "qs": "Claude, reporting my choice", "d": "13 Jun", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 1.0}, {"k": "will", "via": "", "act": "Chooses", "sum": "Makes one fixture the atomic unit of a session.", "q": "Fixture atomic, locked.", "qs": "Claude, reporting my choice", "d": "13 Jun", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 2.0}, {"k": "will", "via": "", "act": "Chooses", "sum": "Chooses a VoltArena landing that forks friend play from the bot ladder.", "q": "Rung 2 it is – a proper VoltArena landing that forks the two modes.", "qs": "Claude, reporting my choice", "d": "13 Jun", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 1.0}, {"k": "will", "via": "", "act": "Chooses", "sum": "Gives each sub-mode its own history; the landing holds only the fork.", "q": "History locked into the sub-modes – each mode owns its own history, nothing on the VoltArena landing but the fork itself.", "qs": "Claude, reporting my choice", "d": "13 Jun", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 1.0}, {"k": "will", "via": "", "act": "Chooses", "sum": "Names the two sides Play vs Friends and Bot League.", "q": "Locked: Play vs Friends and Bot League.", "qs": "Claude, reporting my choice", "d": "13 Jun", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 0.0}, {"k": "claude", "via": "", "act": "Proposes", "sum": "Proposes 3 calibration matches to seed the starting division.", "q": "Placement: 3 calibration matches, unranked, then you're seeded.", "qs": "", "d": "13 Jun", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 0.0}, {"k": "will", "via": "", "act": "Chooses", "sum": "Sets build scope: small real ladder, fixture card over table, numbered divisions, pre-match screen.", "q": "Build focus: playable end-to-end loop, small real ladder (~3 divisions) - My Division: fixture card on top, table beneath - Divisions: numbered, Division 1 = top", "qs": "Claude, reporting my choice", "d": "13 Jun", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 1.0}, {"k": "will", "via": "", "act": "Changes it", "sum": "Replaces calibration with placement from the player's Volt scores.", "q": "That's a much better placement model – lean on the score you already have.", "qs": "Claude, reporting my choice", "d": "14 Jun", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 2.0}, {"k": "will", "via": "", "act": "Changes it", "sum": "Asks for 8 divisions instead of 6.", "q": "I want 8 divisions not 6", "qs": "", "d": "15 Jun", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 1.0}], "gap": "Will's messages in session …aEPbFmr9 (12–15 June) are missing from the transcript; his choices there are known only from Claude's replies."}, {"d": "18 June 2026", "t": "The draining-time sound in Volt and VoltMini is now generative backing music that follows the surge bar - tempo and intensity rise and fall with your remaining charge, each round draws a different instrument, and it pauses at 0x. Mute it with the speaker icon or under Manage in your profile, as before.", "o": "will", "a": "play", "w": 2, "b": "I asked for drain music that speeds up as the bar empties; Claude built the generative engine.", "sh": {"will": 0.625, "claude": 0.375}, "mv": [{"k": "will", "via": "", "act": "Proposes", "sum": "Asks for drain music that speeds up as the surge bar empties.", "q": "I want an audible countdown timer, maybe some light music that gradually gets faster and faster, increasing the sense of urgency audibly as the surge bar decreases to zero", "qs": "", "d": "30 Apr", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 3.0}, {"k": "claude", "via": "", "act": "Offers options", "sum": "Offers a procedural metronome, a speed-modulated loop, or layered stems.", "q": "Procedural metronome (Web Audio API, no assets)", "qs": "", "d": "30 Apr", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 0.0}, {"k": "will", "via": "", "act": "Names the problem", "sum": "Sound set lacks a surge-drain cue.", "q": "we need sounds for surge drain", "qs": "", "d": "10 Jun", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 1.0}, {"k": "claude", "via": "", "act": "Decides while building", "sum": "Adds an accelerating tick that quickens as the bar empties.", "q": "add an accelerating surge-drain tick", "qs": "", "d": "10 Jun", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 0.0}, {"k": "will", "via": "", "act": "Changes it", "sum": "Replaces the tick with a continuous sound rising in pitch or intensity.", "q": "I think we should sonically represent the surge drain with a continuous sound - one that increases in pitch and / or intensity as the bar drains", "qs": "", "d": "12 Jun", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 1.0}, {"k": "will", "via": "", "act": "Proposes", "sum": "Real-instrument music that speeds up with drain; asks for development over time and one swap.", "q": "You all four landed and now you want depth-over-time and one swap.", "qs": "Claude, reporting my choice", "d": "15 Jun", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 1.0}, {"k": "will", "via": "", "act": "Names the problem", "sum": "Hears the tracks as the same melody.", "q": "is it just me or do they all sound like basically the same melody?", "qs": "", "d": "15 Jun", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 1.0}, {"k": "will", "via": "", "act": "Chooses", "sum": "Cuts several instruments and settles the final eight including dulcimer.", "q": "the only new one I'm happy with is dulcimer - let's abandon handpan and harpsichord, and agree on our final eight", "qs": "", "d": "15 Jun", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 1.0}, {"k": "claude", "via": "", "act": "Decides while building", "sum": "Music pauses at 0x and resumes next turn; no repeat instrument until all used.", "q": "pauses at ×0 (and fires the existing `surgeFail`), resuming next turn", "qs": "", "d": "15 Jun", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 2.0}]}, {"d": "18 June 2026", "t": "On the Volt and VoltMini Friends tabs, once you reach your final round, you can see what you need to score that round to overtake any friend who has already finished.", "o": "will", "a": "social", "w": 1, "b": "I asked for a last-round target on the Volt Friends tab; my message is missing from convo.jsonl, so it is quoted from the session log.", "sh": {"will": 1.0}, "e": [{"r": "First raised", "who": "Me", "d": "13 June 2026", "s": "Claude Code", "q": "I want some way on the Volt Friends tab for you to be able to see what you need in the last round to beat a particular friend"}, {"r": "Context", "who": "Claude", "d": "13 June 2026", "s": "Claude Code", "q": "a pure helper computing the minimum final-round score needed to beat a target, given best-K-of-N logic"}, {"r": "Context", "who": "Claude", "d": "13 June 2026", "s": "Claude Code", "q": "Nice work iterating on the copy – it reads much better now."}]}, {"d": "18 June 2026", "t": "In solo games, a word that isn't accepted now clears the input box automatically, so you can type your next attempt straight away.", "o": "will", "a": "play", "w": 1, "b": "I reported that solo kept a rejected word while VoltArena cleared it; my message is missing from convo.jsonl, only Claude's replies survive.", "sh": {"will": 1.0}, "e": [{"r": "First raised", "who": "Claude", "d": "13 June 2026", "s": "Claude Code", "q": "I'll investigate how invalid word submissions are handled in solo vs multiplayer to find where they diverge."}, {"r": "Context", "who": "Claude", "d": "13 June 2026", "s": "Claude Code", "q": "You were right about the inconsistency, and I've fixed it in solo's favour of clearing."}]}, {"d": "13 June 2026", "t": "In Volt and VoltMini, the surge timer no longer pauses when you minimise the window, switch tabs, or reload - the clock keeps running while you're away.", "o": "other", "a": "rules", "w": 1, "b": "A friend found the minimise exploit and I passed on the screenshot; my messages are missing from convo.jsonl, but Claude's replies quote them.", "sh": {"other": 1.0}, "e": [{"r": "First raised", "who": "Claude", "d": "12 June 2026", "s": "Claude Code", "q": "The screenshot describes an exploit: in Volt (the timed mode), if you minimise or background the window, the turn timer appears to pause"}, {"r": "Context", "who": "Claude", "d": "12 June 2026", "s": "Claude Code", "q": "That's the behaviour your friend exploited."}, {"r": "Taken up", "who": "Claude", "d": "12 June 2026", "s": "Claude Code", "q": "Good – the stronger option (survive full reload/close) and all surge modes."}]}, {"d": "13 June 2026", "t": "VoltArena now adds to the monthly donation pot: each player contributes 5p per head-to-head game, up to 6 a day, alongside your Timeless and Volt giving. Games against bots don't count.", "o": "will", "a": "other", "w": 1, "b": "The session opens straight on VoltArena donations with my prompt missing from convo.jsonl; Claude later rebuilt it to my 5p-each idea. No earlier mention.", "sh": {"will": 1.0}, "e": [{"r": "First raised", "who": "Claude", "d": "12 June 2026", "s": "Claude Code", "q": "VoltArena now feeds the donation pot, but only when two humans play each other and finish a game – each such completed match adds 10p."}, {"r": "Decided", "who": "Claude", "d": "12 June 2026", "s": "Claude Code", "q": "Splitting it into \"5p each, contributed individually\" only has an observable effect if that 5p shows up somewhere per-user"}, {"r": "Context", "who": "Claude", "d": "12 June 2026", "s": "Claude Code", "q": "Honestly, your instinct is the right one, and it actually makes the abuse problem *easier*, not harder."}]}, {"d": "12 June 2026", "t": "Gramlet now has sound effects - claiming letters, typing, and submitting words all make a sound, with bigger flourishes for long words and ones that use every letter you own, plus a draining-time sound in the timed modes. Turn it on or off with the new speaker icon during a game, or under Manage in your profile.", "o": "claude", "a": "play", "w": 2, "b": "Claude listed missing sound effects in a March review I didn't answer; I asked for audio myself in April and June without citing it.", "sh": {"will": 0.5, "claude": 0.5}, "mv": [{"k": "claude", "via": "", "act": "Names the problem", "sum": "Notes the game is silent; suggests audio feedback for clicks, accepted words, score reveal.", "q": "No sound effects – Word games benefit from satisfying audio feedback (letter click, word accepted, score reveal). Currently silent.", "qs": "", "d": "23 Mar", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 2.0}, {"k": "will", "via": "", "act": "Proposes", "sum": "Sounds for claim, undo, valid word, full-rack word, nine-letter word, invalid word.", "q": "I want to add sound for my game. when you claim a letter, when you undo a claim, when you submit a word successfully", "qs": "", "d": "10 Jun", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 2.5}, {"k": "claude", "via": "", "act": "Decides while building", "sum": "Synthesised runtime sounds, on by default, with a speaker button and a profile toggle.", "q": "A synthesized sound system – no audio files, everything generated at runtime via the Web Audio API", "qs": "", "d": "10 Jun", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 2.5}, {"k": "will", "via": "", "act": "Changes it", "sum": "More oomph and coverage: surge drain sound, rising typing tones, bigger sounds for longer words.", "q": "we need sounds for surge drain. we need sounds for typing (tone rising on each click?). we need differential sounds for longer words.", "qs": "", "d": "10 Jun", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 2.0}, {"k": "claude", "via": "", "act": "Decides while building", "sum": "Typing climbs a pentatonic scale; word sounds scale with length; full-rack chord stab.", "q": "each added letter plays a rising tone up a pentatonic scale, so building a word plays a satisfying climb.", "qs": "", "d": "10 Jun", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 1.0}, {"k": "will", "via": "", "act": "Changes it", "sum": "Adds delete and cancel sounds, opponent sounds, no auto-submit clash, continuous drain sound.", "q": "I think we should sonically represent the surge drain with a continuous sound - one that increases in pitch and / or intensity as the bar drains", "qs": "", "d": "12 Jun", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 1.0}, {"k": "will", "via": "", "act": "Changes it", "sum": "Drain sound made melodic, added to VoltArena, with a failure sound at x0.", "q": "can we have a slightly more pleasant, melodic noise for surge drain? very grating right now", "qs": "", "d": "12 Jun", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 1.0}, {"k": "claude", "via": "", "act": "Decides while building", "sum": "Moves the speaker toggle off the global header into the in-game pill row.", "q": "Now gameplay-only, in the variant-pill row, right edge", "qs": "", "d": "12 Jun", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 1.0}]}, {"d": "12 June 2026", "t": "The VoltArena scoreboard is now a single tug-of-war bar that slides toward whoever's ahead, instead of 2 separate score totals.", "o": "claude", "a": "social", "w": 1, "b": "Claude proposed the tug-of-war bar in a review I asked for; I had it filed and built, minus the flash.", "sh": {"claude": 1.0}, "e": [{"r": "First raised", "who": "Claude", "d": "10 June 2026", "s": "Claude Code", "q": "Tug-of-war presentation. Render the score as a single bar pushed between two ends rather than two numbers."}, {"r": "Taken up", "who": "Me", "d": "11 June 2026", "s": "Claude Code", "q": "issue 1165 please. no \"LEAD TAKEN\" flash though. just the tug of war at the top"}, {"r": "Context", "who": "Claude", "d": "11 June 2026", "s": "Claude Code", "q": "Filed as [#1165](https://github.com/wjrm500/gramlet/issues/1165) – \"VoltArena: present the match score as a tug-of-war bar with lead-change moments.\""}]}, {"d": "12 June 2026", "t": "Game notifications now split into 2 independent switches in your profile - VoltArena challenges and Friend activity - so you can mute one without the other.", "o": "claude", "a": "social", "w": 1, "b": "Claude flagged the missing per-type switch on 6 June; I asked about granularity on 11 June and Claude proposed the two categories.", "sh": {"claude": 1.0}, "e": [{"r": "First raised", "who": "Claude", "d": "6 June 2026", "s": "Claude Code", "q": "there's no separate on/off switch for just this type of notification yet … I flagged adding a dedicated toggle as a follow-up."}, {"r": "Taken up", "who": "Me", "d": "11 June 2026", "s": "Claude Code", "q": "should we be more granular with notifications permissions"}, {"r": "Context", "who": "Claude", "d": "11 June 2026", "s": "Claude Code", "q": "split the single web-push toggle into two categories, and don't go further than that yet."}]}, {"d": "12 June 2026", "t": "A prompt to turn on notifications now appears at the top of the Friends tab once you've finished today's game.", "o": "will", "a": "social", "w": 1, "b": "I asked for a push nudge after Timeless or Volt and chose the Friends tab for it; Claude had only built one for VoltArena.", "sh": {"will": 1.0}, "e": [{"r": "First raised", "who": "Me", "d": "10 June 2026", "s": "Claude Code", "q": "we need some nudge after completing a game of Timeless or Volt to agree to push notifications"}, {"r": "Decided", "who": "Me", "d": "11 June 2026", "s": "Claude Code", "q": "if it's on the Friends tab for Volt it should be for Timeless too, instead of on the Scorecard."}, {"r": "Context", "who": "Claude", "d": "2 June 2026", "s": "Claude Code", "q": "The end screen already subscribes to presence and shows H2H – ideal home for the post-match prompt."}]}, {"d": "6 June 2026", "t": "The game modes have been renamed to make their family clearer: Voltage is now Volt (the headline timed solo mode), the compact 5x5 beta is now VoltMini, and Multiplayer is now VoltArena - two-player Volt. Scoring, colours, and how each mode plays are all unchanged.", "o": "will", "a": "other", "w": 2, "b": "I started the rename and chose the final names myself after rounds of Claude's suggestions.", "sh": {"will": 0.833, "claude": 0.167}, "mv": [{"k": "will", "via": "", "act": "Proposes", "sum": "Asks to rename Multiplayer to one word signalling two-player Voltage.", "q": "I want to rename multiplayer to something indicating \"Voltage Battle\"", "qs": "", "d": "1 Jun", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 1.5}, {"k": "claude", "via": "", "act": "Offers options", "sum": "Offers Volt-rooted names such as Voltus, VoltX, Revolt and Wattage.", "q": "VoltX – Volt + the \"X\" of versus/crossfire. Modern, gamey, very short.", "qs": "", "d": "1 Jun", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 0.0}, {"k": "will", "via": "", "act": "Chooses", "sum": "Picks Revoltage for the duel mode.", "q": "Revoltage", "qs": "", "d": "1 Jun", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 0.0}, {"k": "will", "via": "", "act": "Chooses", "sum": "Asks for a full internal rename: labels, routes, identifiers and database table.", "q": "Full internal rename – understood. That's a real sweep: labels, routes, identifiers (`multiplayer`/`mpBattle`), CSS classes, filenames, and the `multiplayer_matches` DB table", "qs": "Claude, reporting my choice", "d": "1 Jun", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 1.0}, {"k": "claude", "via": "", "act": "Offers options", "sum": "Offers names for the multiplayer hub's Challenge tab, including Arena.", "q": "New game / Lobby / Arena – more functional / more flavour respectively.", "qs": "", "d": "3 Jun", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 1.0}, {"k": "will", "via": "", "act": "Chooses", "sum": "Chooses Arena as the name of the hub's Challenge tab.", "q": "You chose Arena for the tab.", "qs": "Claude, reporting my choice", "d": "3 Jun", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 1.0}, {"k": "will", "via": "", "act": "Proposes", "sum": "Wants both names to show the link; rename one or both.", "q": "keeping Voltage and renaming Multiplayer to align with that is one option. the other option is renaming both", "qs": "", "d": "5 Jun", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 2.5}, {"k": "claude", "via": "", "act": "Offers options", "sum": "Offers matched pairs, including renaming both to Volt and Bolt.", "q": "Volt & Bolt – rhyming twins.", "qs": "", "d": "5 Jun", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 1.0}, {"k": "will", "via": "", "act": "Chooses", "sum": "Settles: Voltage to Volt, beta Volt to VoltMini, Multiplayer to VoltArena.", "q": "voltage becomes volt. volt becomes VoltMini. multiplayer becomes VoltArena.", "qs": "", "d": "5 Jun", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 3.0}, {"k": "will", "via": "", "act": "Chooses", "sum": "Renames browser URLs with redirects from old paths; stored keys left alone.", "q": "You picked URLs + redirects – and importantly, that's cleanly separable from the DB", "qs": "Claude, reporting my choice", "d": "5 Jun", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 1.0}]}, {"d": "6 June 2026", "t": "VoltArena is Volt played head-to-head - the same 8x8 grid and surge-bar scoring, 1v1 in real time. You and your opponent alternate turns, each racing the surge from 3x down to 0x, and whoever has the higher total after 8 turns each wins. Challenge a friend to build up a head-to-head record, or take on the new Play vs CPU: 5 named bots from a gentle Novice warm-up to a near-flawless Expert. Every match is saved to your History to revisit afterwards.", "o": "will", "a": "rules", "w": 3, "b": "I set VoltArena's direction: Volt rules played head to head, with friend records.", "sh": {"will": 0.81, "claude": 0.19}, "mv": [{"k": "will", "via": "", "act": "Chooses", "sum": "Settles on head-to-head records between friends as the multiplayer's persistence.", "q": "hmm maybe just head to head friend records", "qs": "", "d": "13 May", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 2.0}, {"k": "will", "via": "", "act": "Proposes", "sum": "Proposes aligning multiplayer with the timed solo mode: surge bar, 8x8 grid, auto-submit.", "q": "I want to align the multiplayer experience more closely with Voltage, in terms of surge bar, 8x8 grid, auto-submit word when all letters used", "qs": "", "d": "13 May", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 3.0}, {"k": "claude", "via": "", "act": "Offers options", "sum": "Offers the solo mode with two players, or territory play with surge mechanics layered on.", "q": "(b) Current territory MP with Voltage mechanics layered on.", "qs": "", "d": "13 May", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 1.0}, {"k": "will", "via": "", "act": "Chooses", "sum": "Picks territory play with the turn timer recast as a surge multiplier.", "q": "I'm picturing B. I don't see how it's that big a design fork given individual player turns already have a timer.", "qs": "", "d": "13 May", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 2.0}, {"k": "will", "via": "", "act": "Names the problem", "sum": "Questions how best-of-five rounds can work between two players.", "q": "I'm kind of confused how best of five rounds would work with two players.", "qs": "", "d": "13 May", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 1.0}, {"k": "claude", "via": "", "act": "Proposes", "sum": "Proposes dropping rounds: one continuous game, higher total wins.", "q": "rounds don't really belong in Voltage-aligned territory MP", "qs": "", "d": "13 May", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 2.0}, {"k": "will", "via": "", "act": "Chooses", "sum": "Sets 8 turns each, removes volatile boxes, allows draws, integer scores.", "q": "turn count is 8 yeah … volatile boxes can be removed completely … no tiebreaker, not needed. draw would be acceptable", "qs": "", "d": "13 May", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 2.0}, {"k": "will", "via": "", "act": "Proposes", "sum": "Asks for a CPU opponent so the mode can be played without a second person.", "q": "having a basic (but not completely useless) bot to play against would help me iron out the design quirks", "qs": "", "d": "28 May", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 2.0}, {"k": "will", "via": "", "act": "Proposes", "sum": "Opens CPU play to everyone with five difficulties Novice to Expert, history, maybe personas.", "q": "five difficulty settings - \"Novice\" to \"Expert\" (you can think of names for the three settings between those)", "qs": "", "d": "30 May", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 2.0}, {"k": "claude", "via": "", "act": "Offers options", "sum": "Puts three design calls, with recommendations: difficulty cards, hub layout, CPU games in History.", "q": "Before I build this out, three design decisions are genuinely yours to make. My recommendations are marked.", "qs": "", "d": "30 May", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 1.0}, {"k": "will", "via": "", "act": "Chooses", "sum": "Chooses persona-plus-tier difficulty cards, a compact hub card and picker, CPU games in History.", "q": "Decisions locked: Personas + tier for difficulty cards, compact card + picker for the hub (no new tab), mixed into History", "qs": "Claude, reporting my choice", "d": "30 May", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 1.0}, {"k": "will", "via": "", "act": "Proposes", "sum": "Renames the modes: the multiplayer becomes VoltArena, with split-font branding.", "q": "voltage becomes volt. volt becomes VoltMini. multiplayer becomes VoltArena.", "qs": "", "d": "5 Jun", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 1.0}, {"k": "will", "via": "", "act": "Chooses", "sum": "Sets the VoltArena wordmark: VOLT in the Volt face, ARENA in Bungee, muted neutral.", "q": "So I'll go with your ARENA choice – Bungee + neutral (VOLT in Staatliches/normal colour, ARENA in Bungee in a muted neutral)", "qs": "Claude, reporting my choice", "d": "5 Jun", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 1.0}]}, {"d": "6 June 2026", "t": "Turn on game notifications in your profile to hear about a VoltArena invite from a friend, or a friend finishing today's Timeless or Volt, even when Gramlet is closed.", "o": "will", "a": "social", "w": 2, "b": "I raised push notifications on 2 January relaying a friend's async idea, and asked for friend-finish pings in May; Claude's February list came between.", "sh": {"will": 0.538, "claude": 0.385, "other": 0.077}, "mv": [{"k": "other", "via": "a friend", "act": "Names the problem", "sum": "Relays a friend's idea of an async version with push notifications for a wider audience.", "q": "A friend of mine mentioned that an async version of this game might bring it to a wider audience. I'm guessing he meant like an app with push notifications", "qs": "", "d": "2 Jan", "s": "Claude chat", "wt": 1.0}, {"k": "claude", "via": "", "act": "Proposes", "sum": "Suggests pushes for friend requests, friends beating your score, multiplayer invites.", "q": "Push notifications for friend requests accepted, friends beating your daily score, or multiplayer invites would drive re-engagement.", "qs": "", "d": "27 Feb", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 1.0}, {"k": "claude", "via": "", "act": "Offers options", "sum": "Lists possible notifications: daily reminder, friend accepted, game starting, streak save.", "q": "Realistic notification ideas, ordered by value/effort", "qs": "", "d": "8 May", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 1.0}, {"k": "will", "via": "", "act": "Proposes", "sum": "Asks for a push when a friend finishes Classic or Voltage, score without spoilers, opt-out.", "q": "the first notification I want is when a friend completes either Classic or Voltage game modes. you should get a cool little notification with score but no spoilers.", "qs": "", "d": "8 May", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 3.0}, {"k": "will", "via": "", "act": "Chooses", "sum": "Settles one global profile toggle; friend-finish pushes sent immediately with the score.", "q": "One global toggle in profile settings: \"Notify me when friends finish a game.\" Default off (opt-in). - Send immediately, no spoiler hold-back.", "qs": "Claude, reporting my choice", "d": "8 May", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 2.0}, {"k": "will", "via": "", "act": "Changes it", "sum": "Sends the tap through to that mode's daily hub.", "q": "tap target in notification should be the daily hub for the specific variant", "qs": "", "d": "8 May", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 1.0}, {"k": "claude", "via": "", "act": "Names the problem", "sum": "Notes invites only land when the app is open, since web push is a placeholder.", "q": "Notifications don't leave the app. Email/web-push are placeholders", "qs": "", "d": "31 May", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 2.0}, {"k": "will", "via": "", "act": "Proposes", "sum": "Asks again for friend-finished Timeless or Voltage as a push.", "q": "I want friend finished timeless or voltage as a push", "qs": "", "d": "5 Jun", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 1.0}, {"k": "claude", "via": "", "act": "Decides while building", "sum": "Fires only on a genuine completion and respects the finisher's appear-offline setting.", "q": "I'll also respect the finisher's \"appear offline to friends\" setting, since pushing their activity contradicts hiding it.", "qs": "", "d": "5 Jun", "s": "Claude Code", "wt": 1.0}]}, {"d": "6 June 2026", "t": "You can now download the monthly donation receipt as a PDF straight from the Giving page.", "o": "will", "a": "other", "w": 1, "b": "I asked to upload donation receipts in May and came back for PDF downloads in June.", "sh": {"will": 1.0}, "e": [{"r": "First raised", "who": "Me", "d": "9 May 2026", "s": "Claude Code", "q": "how do I upload a donation receipt for a specific month?"}, {"r": "Decided", "who": "Me", "d": "1 June 2026", "s": "Claude Code", "q": "it should be possible to upload PDF file donation evidence and then for users to download that file"}]}, {"d": "6 June 2026", "t": "VoltMini friend rows now update in real time, both on the hub's Friends tab and when viewing a friend's game - matching Volt.", "o": "will", "a": "social", "w": 1, "b": "I asked for VoltMini to get the live friend updates Volt already had.", "sh": {"will": 1.0}, "e": [{"r": "First raised", "who": "Me", "d": "4 June 2026", "s": "Claude Code", "q": "Volt game mode should allow live updates on friends tab and also on game view, just like Voltage"}, {"r": "Context", "who": "Claude", "d": "4 June 2026", "s": "Claude Code", "q": "Done. Volt now has live updates on both the friends tab and the game view, matching Voltage."}]}, {"d": "23 May 2026", "t": "Mode cards on the home page now show a check badge once you've finished today's Timeless or Voltage game, so you can tell at a glance what's still to play.", "o": "will", "a": "look", "w": 1, "b": "I asked for a played-today sign on the home page; Claude chose the check badge.", "sh": {"will": 1.0}, "e": [{"r": "First raised", "who": "Me", "d": "23 May 2026", "s": "Claude Code", "q": "some indication on home page of whether timeless and voltage have been played today"}, {"r": "Context", "who": "Claude", "d": "23 May 2026", "s": "Claude Code", "q": "render a small check badge overlay on the glyph tile of cards where `played === true`."}]}, {"d": "23 May 2026", "t": "Volt, the compact 5x5 beta (renamed VoltMini in June), now has a Hall of Fame too, ranking its top 100 all-time scores by Game, Round, or Turn.", "o": "will", "a": "social", "w": 1, "b": "I asked for the Voltage Hall of Fame to be copied to the 5x5 mode.", "sh": {"will": 1.0}, "e": [{"r": "First raised", "who": "Me", "d": "23 May 2026", "s": "Claude Code", "q": "voltage has all time stats accessed via trophy emoji.. add the same for Volt"}, {"r": "Context", "who": "Claude", "d": "23 May 2026", "s": "Claude Code", "q": "Everything was already variant-agnostic; the one-line addition to Volt's `routes` is sufficient."}]}, {"d": "23 May 2026", "t": "Fixed a Voltage sync bug where turns played offline could come back with their surge multiplier wiped, silently dropping points off the round score on reconnect.", "o": "will", "a": "rules", "w": 1, "b": "I spotted the lost offline scores on a train; Claude found the cause.", "sh": {"will": 1.0}, "e": [{"r": "First raised", "who": "Me", "d": "21 May 2026", "s": "Claude Code", "q": "offline voltage isn't working, look - the round complete modal shows what I actually scored while battling intermittent signal on the train"}, {"r": "Context", "who": "Claude", "d": "21 May 2026", "s": "Claude Code", "q": "the `'word'` case extracts only `word` from the payload and never passes `chargeFractionAtSubmit` to `submitWord`."}]}, {"d": "23 May 2026", "t": "Swapped the fallback dictionary link on words without a built-in definition from Collins to OneLook (a meta-search across roughly 1000 dictionaries), so rarer entries are far more likely to turn up a result.", "o": "will", "a": "other", "w": 1, "b": "I asked for a better link than Collins and picked OneLook from Claude's options.", "sh": {"will": 1.0}, "e": [{"r": "First raised", "who": "Me", "d": "19 May 2026", "s": "Claude Code", "q": "is there any better place to link than Collins Dictionary, which often misses definitions?"}, {"r": "Decided", "who": "Me", "d": "19 May 2026", "s": "Claude Code", "q": "I quite like the idea of doing OneLook instead"}]}, {"d": "18 May 2026", "t": "Tucked the home page footer links into a burger menu in the centre of the footer. <script>
(() => {
  const root = document.querySelector('.ic');
  if (!root) return;
  const data = JSON.parse(root.querySelector('.ic-data').textContent);
  const sqs = [...root.querySelectorAll('.ic-sq')];
  const detail = root.querySelector('.ic-detail');
  const hint = detail.innerHTML;
  const WHO = { will: 'Me', claude: 'Claude', other: 'Someone else', unknown: 'Can’t tell' };
  const SIZE = { 3: 'Major change', 2: 'Notable change', 1: 'Minor change' };
  const WT = (w) => (w >= 2.5 ? 'Defining' : w >= 1.5 ? 'Substantial' : w >= 0.5 ? 'Detail' : 'Didn’t ship');
  const ORDER = ['will', 'claude', 'other', 'unknown'];
  const state = { area: 'all', mode: 'count' };
  let current = null;
  const el = (tag, cls, text) => {
    const n = document.createElement(tag);
    if (cls) n.className = cls;
    if (text) n.textContent = text;
    return n;
  };
  // Largest-remainder rounding, so the shown percentages always add up to 100.
  const pcts = (vals) => {
    const t = vals.reduce((a, b) => a + b, 0) || 1;
    const raw = vals.map((v) => (100 * v) / t);
    const out = raw.map(Math.floor);
    let left = 100 - out.reduce((a, b) => a + b, 0);
    raw.map((v, i) => [v - Math.floor(v), i]).sort((a, b) => b[0] - a[0]).forEach(([, i]) => { if (left > 0 && vals[i] > 0) { out[i]++; left--; } });
    return out;
  };
  const inMode = (x) => state.mode !== 'major' || x.w === 3;
  const weight = (x) => (state.mode === 'weight' ? x.w : 1);
  const credit = (xs) => {
    const c = { will: 0, claude: 0, other: 0, unknown: 0 };
    let t = 0;
    for (const x of xs) {
      for (const k in x.sh) c[k] += weight(x) * x.sh[k];
      t += weight(x);
    }
    return [c, t];
  };
  function show(sq) {
    if (current) current.classList.remove('is-on');
    current = sq;
    if (!sq) { detail.innerHTML = hint; return; }
    sq.classList.add('is-on');
    const x = data[sq.dataset.i];
    detail.replaceChildren();
    const ks = ORDER.filter((k) => x.sh[k]);
    const ps = pcts(ks.map((k) => x.sh[k]));
    const split = ks.map((k, j) => WHO[k] + ' ' + ps[j] + '%').join(' · ');
    detail.append(el('p', 'ic-meta', x.d + ' · ' + SIZE[x.w]), el('p', 'ic-text', x.t), el('p', 'ic-split', split));
    if (x.mv) {
      const dots = el('p', 'ic-dots');
      for (const m of x.mv) {
        const d = el('i', 'ic-dot ic-' + m.k);
        d.style.setProperty('--s', (6 + 4 * m.wt) + 'px');
        dots.append(d);
      }
      detail.append(dots);
      if (x.gap) detail.append(el('p', 'ic-src', x.gap));
      const list = el('ol', 'ic-ev');
      for (const m of x.mv) {
        const li = el('li', 'ic-' + m.k);
        li.append(el('span', 'ic-role', [m.d, WHO[m.k] + (m.via ? ' (via ' + m.via + ')' : ''), m.act, WT(m.wt)].join(' · ')));
        li.append(el('span', 'ic-sum', m.sum), el('span', 'ic-q', '“' + m.q + '”'));
        li.append(el('span', 'ic-src', [m.qs, m.s].filter(Boolean).join(', ')));
        list.append(li);
      }
      detail.append(list);
    } else {
      if (x.b) detail.append(el('p', 'ic-basis', x.b));
      const list = el('ol', 'ic-ev');
      for (const v of x.e || []) {
        const li = el('li');
        li.append(el('span', 'ic-role', v.r), el('span', 'ic-q', '“' + v.q + '”'), el('span', 'ic-src', [v.who, v.d, v.s].filter(Boolean).join(', ')));
        list.append(li);
      }
      detail.append(list);
      detail.append(el('p', 'ic-src', 'A minor change: credited whole to whoever raised it.'));
    }
  }
  function render() {
    for (const s of sqs) {
      const x = data[s.dataset.i];
      s.classList.toggle('is-dim', !inMode(x) || (state.area !== 'all' && x.a !== state.area));
    }
    const live = data.filter((x) => inMode(x) && (state.area === 'all' || x.a === state.area));
    const [c, t] = credit(live);
    const hp = pcts(ORDER.map((k) => c[k]));
    ['will', 'claude', 'other'].forEach((k) => {
      root.querySelector(`[data-k="${k}"]`).textContent = (t ? hp[ORDER.indexOf(k)] : 0) + '%';
    });
    for (const bar of root.querySelectorAll('.ic-bar[data-area]')) {
      const [bc, bt] = credit(data.filter((x) => inMode(x) && x.a === bar.dataset.area));
      const track = bar.querySelector('.ic-bar-track');
      track.replaceChildren();
      for (const k of ORDER) {
        if (bc[k] > 0) { const seg = el('span', 'ic-seg-' + k); seg.style.flex = bc[k]; track.append(seg); }
      }
      bar.querySelector('.ic-bar-val').textContent = bt ? pcts(ORDER.map((k) => bc[k]))[1] + '%' : '–';
    }
  }
  const press = (sel, btn) => root.querySelectorAll(sel).forEach((b) => b.setAttribute('aria-pressed', String(b === btn)));
  root.addEventListener('click', (ev) => {
    const sort = ev.target.closest('.ic-sort');
    if (sort) {
      press('.ic-sort', sort);
      const key = sort.dataset.sort === 'date' ? 'do' : 'so';
      sqs.forEach((s) => { s.style.order = s.dataset[key]; });
      return;
    }
    const mode = ev.target.closest('.ic-mode');
    if (mode) { press('.ic-mode', mode); state.mode = mode.dataset.mode; render(); return; }
    const chip = ev.target.closest('.ic-chip');
    if (chip) { press('.ic-chip', chip); state.area = chip.dataset.area; render(); return; }
    const sq = ev.target.closest('.ic-sq');
    if (sq) show(sq);
  });
  root.addEventListener('pointerover', (ev) => {
    const sq = ev.target.closest('.ic-sq');
    if (sq && ev.pointerType === 'mouse') show(sq);
  });
  root.addEventListener('focusin', (ev) => {
    const sq = ev.target.closest('.ic-sq');
    if (sq) show(sq);
  });
  render();
})();
</script>

<ul class="ic-find"><li><b>76%</b> of the threads were opened by me.</li><li>I made <b>76%</b> of the defining moves, and <b>63%</b> of the detail ones.</li><li>Claude’s proposals shipped as proposed <b>57%</b> of the time (253); mine <b>87%</b> (316).</li><li>The two raters gave the same score to <b>86%</b> of moves, and were within one point on <b>100%</b>.</li></ul>

### I trained it, and it trained me

Two things changed over the year that I didn’t expect.

First, the AI’s personality. Back in January I was already asking it to say less:

> That’s a lot of text, please summarise questions you actually need answering succinctly

It didn’t, really. By June and July its replies were averaging 260 to 280 words, about 20 words of Claude for every word of mine. So on 31 July I added a section to the project’s instructions file, `CLAUDE.md`, on how to talk to me, including the line: “The maintainer has ADHD. A dense reply gets abandoned, not skimmed.” Within three weeks its replies had halved. By September they were down to around 95 words. Meanwhile the “You’re right!”s dropped from 7% of its replies to under 2%, and it started disagreeing with me more often. When it did push back, I accepted its argument about two times in three. When I didn’t, I wasn’t subtle about it:

> surge doesn’t “build” - please go away and actually learn how the game works before you try to write content

![A line chart of median words per message from December to October. Claude’s replies climb from about 120 words to 279 in July, then fall to about 95 after the 31 July rule. My messages fall steadily from 57 words to 11.](./words.svg)
_Median words per message: mine, and Claude’s replies_

Second, the instructions file itself. `CLAUDE.md` is where you tell Claude how to behave in your project, and I kept adding to it every time it did something annoying. By 12 August it was **42,000 words long**, which is nearly as long as _The Great Gatsby_. It contained rules about rules. The next day I had it cut to 16,000 words, and within four weeks it had grown back to 34,000. There was a whole separate file, `DECISIONS.md`, because AI review passes kept re-raising questions I’d already settled; one issue was re-filed nineteen hours after I closed it. On 9 September we cut it down to about 3,500 words, and this time it stayed cut.

![A line chart of the word count of CLAUDE.md from February to October. It creeps up to about 8,000 words by late July, shoots up to 42,477 on 12 August, drops to 15,970 the next day, climbs back to 33,861 by 8 September, then falls to 3,508 on 9 September.](./claude-md.svg)
_Words in `CLAUDE.md`, with Gatsby for scale_

What didn’t change was who decided. Month after month, Claude’s share of the credit in the census above sat at about a fifth, even as my messages shrank to a dozen words. What did change was how well its suggestions landed: in January, 13 of the 32 it made shipped as it proposed them; from June to August, 80 of 130 did. Only in September and October, with just 11 changes between them, does its share start to climb.

![A stacked column chart of who gets the credit for the changes shipped each month. My share sits between 73% and 80% from January to August, and Claude’s between 13% and 24%. September and October, drawn paler because they hold only 3 and 8 changes, show Claude at 35% and 47%.](./credit-by-month.svg)
_Who gets the credit for each month’s changes_

Turns out the most important code I wrote this year wasn’t code at all; it was a document about how to talk to me, which I then had to heavily edit because it had become impossible to talk to.

(For the record, I also swore at it about 12 times per thousand messages between January and May, and not once in September or October. I’d like to say that’s personal growth. It’s probably just that it got better.)

### Was it me, or the models?

There’s a complication I’ve been ignoring. I didn’t talk to one AI this year. I talked to ten.

![A chart with one row per Claude model, from Sonnet 4.5 in December to Opus 5.5 in October, and one small line for every session I ran on it. Beside each model are my median words per message, falling from 34 to 11, how often I said I was confused, per 100 messages, highest for Opus 5 at 2.1, Claude’s share of the credit in that model’s sessions, from 23% for Opus 4.7 to 42% for Fable 5, and how often its suggestions shipped as it proposed them, highest for Opus 4.8 at 70%.](./models.svg)
_Every session, by the model it ran on_

My messages shrank with almost every new model: a median of 34 words with Sonnet 4.5, 26 with Opus 4.6, 20 with 4.7, 15 with 4.8 and 11 with Opus 5.5. How often I told it to merge went from about 3% of my messages with Opus 4.5 to 16% with 4.8 and 26% with Sonnet 5. And the flattery faded: Sonnet 4.5 told me I was right in one reply in ten; Opus 5.5 has managed it in about one in seventy.

The last two columns come from the census. Claude’s share of the credit moved around more than my words did, from 23% in Opus 4.7’s sessions to 42% in Fable 5’s, and Opus 4.8’s suggestions were the ones I kept most often, seven times in ten. Opus 5.5 is on 51%, but from only two changes so far.

My memory is that Opus 5, which arrived on 24 July, was the one that drove me up the wall. The data says I swore at it less than at any other big model, but told it I was confused more often than any other. It wasn’t even verbose. Its median reply was 158 words, against 254 for Opus 4.8. It just used them to say things I didn’t understand.

So did I trust it more because it got better, or did it get better because I wrote 42,000 words of rules at it? Honestly, both, and they’re hard to pull apart. The ADHD rule went in on 31 July, a week after Opus 5 arrived, and in an Opus 5 session at that. What I can say is that each new model got a slightly lazier version of me, and coped fine.

### So can I call myself the creator?

I think so, yes, as long as I’m careful about the verb.

I can’t say I _built_ Gramlet, or _coded_ it. I typed 115 lines in eight months; the AI typed the rest, and plenty of the good ideas came out of our back-and-forth rather than out of my head alone. But I did invent the game, by hand, in 2022. I sparked most of what it’s become, I chose between every set of options, and nothing hit production without me saying so. Gramlet is a Will May game.

### Final thoughts

In December I wrote that I no longer knew where I ended and the AI began. Having now been through ten months of data with a fine-tooth comb, I think I actually do know, and the answer is weirdly comforting.

The AI made building almost free. A feature that would once have taken me a month of evenings now takes a message from my phone and a “merge if you’re happy”. And when building is free, the only things left that matter are the things the building was always in service of: having ideas, choosing between them, and knowing when something isn’t right yet. Twenty of my 29 ideas arrived after nine at night or in the small hours. That’s the job now.

Anyway, go and play [Gramlet](https://gramlet.co.uk). The surge bar was my idea. 🟨⚡
